# Reviewed plan handoff: f-20261001-14

## Scope and adoption boundary

Pinned singleton finding f-20261001-14, derived impact class unclassified, selected by Felix's explicit pin. Entry remains build because the failed-child recovery and failure-observation ownership contract was unsettled. Base d445b1280ac4061aa1c54506a500bcfa6c827551. Plan: tasks/plans/2026-10-13-retain-failed-engine-cleanup.md. Only f-20261001-14 is assigned. Co-located inline/lens joiners remain open and are not part of this planner's specification.

This is a complete plan-only handoff to the drain publisher, not an implementation closure. No build lock, source or ledger edit, commit, push, server, browser or test/gate execution occurred. Read-only findings check passed. The checkout stayed clean at the assigned base. The adopter must record all autonomous decisions before implementation, including the explicit partial correction to d-20260901-18, then implement O1–O5 together through one sensitive write leaf and obtain all stated proofs/reviews/gates and release evidence.

## Fixed request and source evidence

The complete fixed mandate is preserved verbatim in the plan, including the production game-loop cleanup annotation from 2026-10-09. Independent Locate source-traced the actor/pending ownership loss and actual game loop. Source tracing confirms that the child control is consumed after unconfirmed reap, pending failure entries are removed, natural game completion discards the live-session owner, and shutdown rescans surviving failed sessions. The frozen Traced premises block and retained probe-1-r1.txt provide exact paths. No runtime measurement was made here.

## Autonomous decisions for the adopter

Optimal: retain native cleanup authority with bounded explicit retries → I recommend this approach.

Decided autonomously for adopter recording:

* Keep the existing pending registry as the reaping owner, extending its failed-state contract. Reject a second registry, unlimited automatic retries and mere failed-actor retention after its child handle was dropped. Reverse by substituting a proven shared cleanup owner with the same O1–O5 semantics.
* Retain the child handle after unconfirmed cleanup. This changes d-20260901-18's drop-after-timeout clause and its keep-actor rejection, with new evidence: the actor must still exit, but cleanup authority can live independently, and this finding demonstrates lost ownership after that drop. Keep its bounded waits and rejection of detached unbounded waits. The adopter records the partial correction explicitly before implementation.
* Preserve d-20261006-16/19's pending owner, cancellation independence and lossless result sharing. Extend them to multiple explicit attempts after failure, justified by this finding's unconfirmed cleanup. Preserve d-20261007-17/19's exact-generation and Kill handoff rules. Preserve d-20261008-10/11 preemption and stable requester error semantics. Refine d-20261008-12 by separating the original actor ending result from later child cleanup attempts, never overwrite what an attached original caller observed.
* Reserve 4096 owned-generation slots before spawning to bound non-evictable cleanup owners. Reject failure-only insertion with eviction or refusal after a child exists. This is a native safety bound, with no setting or extra tool. Reverse by replacing reservation with an equally proven finite ownership bound.
* Retain the real game-loop outcome on its live session. Reject log-only failure and relying on a later empty registry scan. Reverse by making the actual loop's joined return value equivalently carry cleanup failure to every session owner.
* Replace raw actor/game JoinError interpolation with a shared stable public conversion and native-only detailed logs. This corrects an evidenced existing leak at both integrations rather than preserving it as part of error compatibility. The serialized shape and existing category remain stable. Reject a new public error variant or duplicate join mappers. Reversal would require an equally proven public/native diagnostic separation, never reintroducing panic text into serialized messages.

No Felix product decision is pending. These choices repair the existing teardown contract, without adding a user workflow.

Code-quality and root-cause are marked plan-review false and were excluded here, remaining code-review obligations.

* **I1 — Raw task panic diagnostics can reach the renderer.** Witness: review-tauri-security, round 1, raw finding 1, blocker, confidence 98. Claim: game loop join at game.rs:1181 and engine actor join at process.rs:4338 format JoinError directly into Conflict, which serializes its Display text. Source confirmation: Tokio 1.50.0 Display embeds a panic string and Error serialization uses to_string. Disposition: Fix. Failing obligation: MANDATE's renderer failure communication under the IPC/native diagnostic rules, implemented by O5. Authority: the fixed mandate asks how the renderer learns cleanup could not be confirmed, with native diagnostic egress prohibited by the existing async/IPC boundary. Correction: one shared safe conversion at both task-join boundaries, native detailed logs and public-marker exclusion tests. Dependencies: O2 shared join/result semantics, O4 retirement propagation, O5 command and composite serialization. Closure: pending round 2 review-plan and security witness on the final candidate.
* **Root proof correction, no lens finding:** project push mapping requires real-product `pnpm verify:app` for lifecycle/process teardown and all three gate flags for any main.rs change. Added the existing release-build receipt route and real-app proof, with explicit live-engine limitation, plus consumer/error test selectors and corrected final gate selection. No new tool or verifier. Round 2 includes review-plan, tests and platform semantics to judge the affected verification contract.

## Original adopted finding

Raw report claim, unchanged:

[blocker] src-tauri/src/game.rs:1181 — A game-loop panic is formatted directly into `Error::Conflict`. When replacing that session, `start_game` can return the error to the renderer because `Error::Serialize` uses `self.to_string()` at `src-tauri/src/error.rs:959`. Locked Tokio 1.50.0 includes the string panic payload in `JoinError::Display`, exposing raw native panic diagnostics and any embedded paths. The same leak exists for engine actor joins at `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:4338`, reachable through Kill and retirement commands. O5 must replace these raw join messages with stable public text while retaining diagnostics natively, and verify that panic markers cannot enter serialized results. Preserving the existing mapping preserves this leak. (confidence: 98)

I1 has one witness: review-tauri-security, round 1, finding ordinal 1. Review-plan and that witness explicitly closed it in round 2. No issue was skipped, deferred, withdrawn, carried, merged into a hidden issue or left open. No new finding was filed because the adopted native diagnostic defect is a same-area obligation of this plan.

### probe-1-r1.txt

The failure survives in existing actor/entry references, but becomes undiscoverable through the supervisor after pending cleanup completes, and the production game loop discards its returned error.

Source-confirmed premises, with no runtime measurements:

- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:984` — Termination already attempts bounded graceful exit followed by force-kill and reap, so the remaining error represents cleanup that could not be confirmed.
- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:3633` — The runtime records its terminal reap outcome in shared actor state, and `EngineActor::terminate` combines that recorded outcome with the shared task join at line 4288.
- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2410` — Exact cleanup transfers a published generation from `actors` into `pending_actors` before awaiting termination.
- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2768` — One cancellation-independent pending task calls `EngineActor::terminate`, stores `Result<(), Arc<Error>>`, and removes the pending entry regardless of success or failure.
- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2794` — Owners already holding the pending entry continue receiving its stored failure through `Error::Shared`.
- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2175` — Exact termination preserves an actor observed before the lifecycle wait, but a caller arriving after registry removal has no observed actor and receives `Ok` from `reap_published` at line 2455.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:303` — `RegisteredGameEngine` already retains the exact actor, key and generation, but `terminate_game_engines` at line 1428 passes only key and generation to the supervisor.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:3111` — The production loop logs cleanup failure, completes the session and returns `()`, leaving its `JoinHandle<()>` unable to communicate that failure.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1207` — `finish_retired` joins the loop and unconditionally repeats exact cleanup, which can return success after the loop’s failed reap removed both registry entries.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1723` — Replacement propagates retirement failure and abandons newly constructed engines, but the preceding loss path lets retirement return success and replacement proceed.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1536` — Natural completion removes the live session without retaining its cleanup outcome, so later `shutdown_all` may have no game entry to inspect.
- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2604` — Supervisor drains preserve failures for actors or pending entries captured in their snapshot, but cannot discover a failed generation removed before that snapshot.
- `src-tauri/src/main.rs:1822` — Application shutdown concurrently runs supervisor and game drains and reports failure if either returns an error, making observation depend on whether either retained the failed generation.
- `src-tauri/src/main.rs:2377` — `ExitRequested` invokes that shutdown core, logs its failures internally and exits unconditionally afterward.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:2084` — Committed abort deliberately logs retirement failure and returns `Ok`, so observing cleanup failure internally must remain distinct from changing abort’s public result.

Design recommendation:

**Optimal: retain one exact-generation terminal outcome through ownership handoff → I recommend that contract.**

- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2744` — Extend the existing pending/result ownership so completion does not erase an unobserved failure, with repeatable reads and supervisor discoverability until the required owning paths have observed it.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1428` — Route game cleanup through a supervisor operation accepting the existing exact actor reference alongside key and generation, allowing `finish_retired` to recover the recorded outcome after registry removal.
- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2604` — Make shutdown include retained completed failures once per drain invocation, avoiding an endless loop over terminal entries and preventing one reader from consuming another owner’s evidence.
- `src-tauri/src/error.rs:153` — Reuse `Error::Shared` for lossless internal delivery and retain existing safe game/IPC aggregation.
- `tasks/decisions.md:5904` — Preserve separate registration and pending guard roles, exact-generation targeting, cancellation-independent shared termination and exchange preemption, while treating retention after completion as the explicitly deferred f-20261001-14 extension.
- `tasks/findings.md:12925` — Define bounded retention and acknowledgement before implementation, since silent eviction of an unobserved failure would recreate the defect and result retention alone does not provide a child-reap retry.

Current test seams and missing coverage:

- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:3913` — `failing_terminate_test_actor`, termination-reply gates and drain-snapshot gates provide deterministic failed-reap and observation-order seams.
- `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:13060` — Existing tests cover queued/later actor terminators, exact callers already observing an actor and drains whose snapshots precede another owner’s failed reap.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:7865` — The clean-join retirement failure test uses `published_teardown_fixture`, whose loop explicitly skips engine cleanup at line 4146, so it exercises fallback termination rather than production result loss.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:6368` — Parallel game/supervisor shutdown coverage uses successful termination and does not establish failed-result delivery.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:6929` — Never-started loop failure coverage checks logging and retirement, but does not ask a subsequent retirement or shutdown caller to recover the failure.
- `src-tauri/src/game.rs:3111` — Missing coverage must execute the real started loop’s failed cleanup before normal join, replacement, shutdown snapshots and natural session removal, then assert each owning caller receives failure while unrelated/newer generations survive.

Inspected the four named source files, finding, specified decisions and rules, plus read-only git status, which was clean. No writes, tests, gates, servers or browsers were run.
### plan-r1.md

# Plan: Retain failed engine cleanup ownership

## Goal

Resolve f-20261001-14. A failed engine teardown stays discoverable and retryable by its exact native generation. Game replacement and application shutdown cannot report successful cleanup after the production game loop lost a failed reap.

## MANDATE

### A failed engine termination drops the actor from the supervisor, so no later owner can retry or confirm cleanup

* **ID:** f-20261001-14 · **Status:** open · **Area:** engine-uci · **Root:** - · **Entry:** build · **Blocked:** none
* **Where:** `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:1448-1461` (`terminate_exact`: `let result = current.actor.terminate().await; self.actors.remove(key); result`), used by `retire_engine`, `retire_executables`, `kill_engine`, `kill_engines` and tab termination.
* **Defect:** when `actor.terminate()` returns an error, the actor is removed from the registry anyway. The child process may still be alive (kill-on-drop is best effort), and nothing — tab close, app shutdown via `RunEvent::ExitRequested`, a later retirement — can find it again.
* **Why it matters:** `.claude/rules/async-resource-invariants.md` requires cleanup on every exit path; an engine with a large hash (8 GB in Felix's configuration) can outlive its owner unseen.
* **Open question:** keep failed actors in a bounded "reaping" set that shutdown and later retirements retry, or make termination escalate (kill, wait with deadline) until it is confirmed before removal — and how the renderer learns that a process could not be confirmed dead.
* **Found by:** Codex `review-engine-protocol` lens, round 2 of the Stockfish 19 upgrade plan review (`tasks/plans/2026-10-01-stockfish-19-upgrade.md`, issue R2 retirement-failure), confirmed by the orchestrator from source, 2026-10-01.
* **Additional review evidence (2026-10-09):** f-20260926-07 cumulative review traced the normally joined production game loop. src-tauri/src/game.rs logs a terminate_game_engines failure at the loop tail and returns (). Shared pending teardown subsequently removes the failed generation, so finish_retired's unconditional exact termination finds no registered or pending owner and returns Ok. This can make replacement and shutdown_all report success after an unconfirmed reap. Existing clean-join failure tests use a loop that skips engine cleanup and do not exercise that production loss path. The cause is this finding's failed-actor/result retention contract, not an empty-join cancellation gap. Preserve the completed outcome or failed generation until the owning game/shutdown path has observed failure. Verification for this finding must include the production-loop cleanup path, not only direct fallback teardown. The separate design question remains open at build tier. review-error-handling confidence 95, adopting drain a51461c5-317b-40c0-8297-ab16a2fc6904.

## Threat model and non-goals

Ordinary user actions overlap teardown, replacement, tab close, engine removal and shutdown. An engine can be silent, exit unexpectedly, or fail an OS kill or wait. Callers can be cancelled while teardown proceeds. Support Linux, macOS and Windows. A permanently unkillable OS process cannot be guaranteed dead within a finite deadline, so retain its authority and report the unconfirmed outcome honestly.

Do not add an adversarial child-descendant threat model, PID-based signalling, new renderer controls, IPC events, persistent cleanup journals, configurable retry policy, or a background retry service. Warm-search ownership, closed-tab admission and post-shutdown late publication remain their separately recorded work. Planner co-located joiners are not planned here.

## Traced premises

Base: d445b1280ac4061aa1c54506a500bcfa6c827551. Entry revalidation: build remains required. The actor's task completion is distinct from confirming the child exited, and recovery ownership plus failure observation needs a shared contract.

* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:984-1026`: termination already bounds quit/write/wait and force-kill/reap. More escalation alone cannot guarantee success.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:762-798,1207-1213`: `ChildUciIo` owns the child control and consumes it for termination, including failure. Keeping only a finished actor cannot retry its OS wait.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2171-2222,2409-2457`: exact termination hands registered actors to pending ownership, with generation checks and preemption before lifecycle waiting.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2604-2659,2733-2814`: drains scan registered and pending actors, but the entry-owned termination task removes a pending entry for both success and failure. Leaving a failed entry there without changing the drain loop would spin forever.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:3634-3651,4034-4049,4288-4348`: the first actor teardown outcome and shared task join are already retained per actor. They are not a retryable child owner or a supervisor-discoverable failed-generation record.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2119,3220-3275`: the production interactive and shared registered launch paths spawn before installing pending ownership. Slot reservation and cleanup transfer must precede child creation rather than refuse ownership after it.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:3389-3444`: incomplete child IO construction has bounded cleanup too. A failed cleanup cannot be discarded merely because no complete actor exists yet.
* `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1147-1215,1428-1464,3111-3121`: the real game loop only logs engine cleanup failure, while retirement later retries exact registrations and may see no entry. Its joined task returns unit.
* `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1536-1563,2021-2055`: natural completion removes a session from `games`. Shutdown rescans failed sessions without retiring them, so a retained failure also needs a once-per-session drain rule.
* `src-tauri/src/main.rs:1720,1823-1844`: exit runs game and supervisor teardown concurrently inside a 15-second process budget. No sequential retry sweep may extend that budget.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:11120-11133`: an existing test locks in removal after a failed teardown and needs to assert failed-owner retention instead.

## Approach

### O1 — Own the child until confirmed reaped

One shared cleanup owner carries the original child handle, executable/resource leases and exact generation across runtime teardown and actor-task exit. Route initial graceful teardown and later forced cleanup through the existing bounded child termination domain primitive. Successful OS wait is the condition that releases the child authority. Failed kill, failed wait, timeout, cancelled requester and incomplete IO construction preserve a supervisor-reachable cleanup owner. Keep protocol service permanently closed after termination starts. Actor completion must not hold a request open waiting for an unbounded reaper.

Verification level: deterministic child-control tests plus a real child fixture. After an injected failed wait, assert the same owned child can be reaped on a later bounded attempt and that successful reaping releases ownership. Keeping the old consume-and-drop implementation must fail this assertion. Test partial IO construction through its production cleanup boundary.

### O2 — Retain failed generations and share each attempt

Use the existing generation-keyed pending ownership, without a second cleanup registry. A failed attempt leaves its entry discoverable, cancelled and unusable for new UCI work. All exact and matching teardown operations, replacement cleanup, rejected publication and dropped-owner cleanup use this ownership. A later explicit teardown invocation may start one bounded retry of a completed failed attempt. Concurrent callers attached to one attempt receive that attempt's identical result, even if a later attempt recovers. Requester cancellation cannot cancel the owned attempt. Retain actor-join failure separately from child-reap confirmation so a successful child retry does not erase an actor task error.

Each drain processes a generation at most once per invocation, including generations handed off while scanning. A failed retained entry cannot cause an immediate repeat or infinite loop. Preserve the first generation's identity if the same key acquires a newer actor. Exact cleanup never touches another generation. Matching tab/id/executable drains include older failed entries even after renderer removal. Remove the pending entry only after confirmed child cleanup and safe terminal-result publication to attached owners.

Verification level: supervisor integration tests. A failed exact teardown followed by a tab/global/identity drain must return the retained failure or the result of a real bounded retry, and the owner remains until confirmation. Controlled concurrent terminators and cancelled waiters must observe one attempt, with original error category, display, diagnostic and root failure intact. A repeated-failure drain must return within its deadline and leave ownership. Same-name/path engines and a newer generation must remain independent.

### O3 — Bound ownership without evicting unresolved children

Reserve capacity before every production supervisor-owned child creation. The native limit is 4096 concurrently owned child generations, including active, spawning and unresolved cleanup. Capacity follows the child rather than a mutable key or replaceable admission. Full capacity refuses a new spawn with the existing renderer-safe `ResourceLimit`, without creating a child or deleting a failed owner. An admission abandoned before spawn releases its reservation. After spawning, only confirmed cleanup releases the reservation. No eviction or expiration of a live or unconfirmed child. This bound prevents an endless sequence of failed retirements accumulating handles and processes.

Verification level: capacity tests with a smaller test-only limit. Assert refusal happens before the production spawn boundary, unresolved teardown keeps its slot, pre-spawn cancellation returns its slot, and confirmed reaping makes capacity available. Exercise concurrent admission and key replacement so map removal cannot prematurely return capacity.

### O4 — Preserve the production game cleanup outcome

The live game session retains the production loop's engine cleanup outcome, including on natural completion. Retirement, replacement and shutdown that own the session observe that outcome even if a later supervisor retry succeeds or the loop has already joined. Combine it with fallback cleanup and join failures using the existing shared error representation. Both game sides are attempted. Concurrent game and supervisor shutdown must share generation teardown without consuming each other's failure. Natural completion keeps an unobserved failed session reachable in existing game ownership, while completed-game snapshots and events keep their current meaning. The first completed retirement observation may release this failed-session ownership, even when returning the failure, because unresolved children remain supervisor-owned under O2. Callers already holding the session still receive its retained result. Bound unobserved failed-session retention at 4096, reserving its place before game launch and refusing overflow before resources are created, without silently pruning failures. Game shutdown processes a captured session once per invocation and cannot rescan a failed session indefinitely.

Verification level: actual game-loop integration tests. Drive the production loop through its cleanup tail with injected reap failure, then retire/replace/shut down. Assert the owning caller receives that failure, including after a successful later retry. Keep normal natural completion, join timeout/abort and stale-session replacement green. A stand-in loop that skips production cleanup cannot satisfy this obligation.

### O5 — Keep existing error and exit contracts

Direct renderer commands return the current stable error mapping for unconfirmed cleanup. Native logs carry generation and diagnostic context, while OS paths and raw diagnostic details stay native. No new binding, event, UI copy or capability scope is required. Verify the existing consumer paths for Kill, tab close, removal and game replacement rather than creating another notification route. Global shutdown reports failure through its existing aggregation and remains within `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET`, even with retained failed entries and concurrent game cleanup.
Committed game abort retains its existing public `Ok` contract after the abort has committed. Its cleanup failure remains logged and supervisor-owned, without making the already committed action appear uncommitted.

Verification level: existing command/error mapping tests and native shutdown integration tests. Assert unconfirmed cleanup cannot produce `Ok` or a successful native shutdown result, and that repeated failures do not extend the bounded process exit path. Use cross-platform deterministic child controls for the ownership protocol, real process proof on Unix, Windows GNU compile locally and the required Windows/macOS CI runtime jobs before release.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Optimal: retain native cleanup authority with bounded explicit retries → I recommend this approach.

Decided autonomously for adopter recording:

* Keep the existing pending registry as the reaping owner, extending its failed-state contract. Reject a second registry, unlimited automatic retries and mere failed-actor retention after its child handle was dropped. Reverse by substituting a proven shared cleanup owner with the same O1–O5 semantics.
* Retain the child handle after unconfirmed cleanup. This changes d-20260901-18's drop-after-timeout clause and its keep-actor rejection, with new evidence: the actor must still exit, but cleanup authority can live independently, and this finding demonstrates lost ownership after that drop. Keep its bounded waits and rejection of detached unbounded waits. The adopter records the partial correction explicitly before implementation.
* Preserve d-20261006-16/19's pending owner, cancellation independence and lossless result sharing. Extend them to multiple explicit attempts after failure, justified by this finding's unconfirmed cleanup. Preserve d-20261007-17/19's exact-generation and Kill handoff rules. Preserve d-20261008-10/11 preemption and stable requester error semantics. Refine d-20261008-12 by separating the original actor ending result from later child cleanup attempts, never overwrite what an attached original caller observed.
* Reserve 4096 owned-generation slots before spawning to bound non-evictable cleanup owners. Reject failure-only insertion with eviction or refusal after a child exists. This is a native safety bound, with no setting or extra tool. Reverse by replacing reservation with an equally proven finite ownership bound.
* Retain the real game-loop outcome on its live session. Reject log-only failure and relying on a later empty registry scan. Reverse by making the actual loop's joined return value equivalently carry cleanup failure to every session owner.

No Felix product decision is pending. These choices repair the existing teardown contract, without adding a user workflow.

## Risks / open questions

* Some OS waits never confirm exit. A bounded retry returns failure and retains ownership until another explicit cleanup or process exit. Do not claim success from `start_kill` or kill-on-drop.
* Actor join errors and original cleanup errors must remain observable after a recovery. Avoid reusing a first-outcome `OnceLock` as a mutable retry result.
* Child handles and executable/resource leases must move together. Reaping by a saved PID is forbidden because identity can be reused.
* Planner proof is source tracing and adversarial review only. Runtime tests, fault-revert evidence, gates and platform CI are obligations for implementation, not results claimed here.

## Not part of this task

Co-located findings, f-20261006-04 late publication, unrelated protocol timing, renderer storage, engine-cache behaviour, legal texts, deployment and installer changes. The adopter handles eligible joiners after this plan under next-finding, with their own proofs.

## Phases

One cohesive engine-cleanup ownership phase. Child recovery, pending attempts, capacity and game-result observation must land together because their ownership/result contracts are coupled. There are no independent dependency waves to split across overlapping source files.

* **Ownership:** one selected-executor workspace-write leaf, role `sensitive`, with a self-contained reviewed brief and no root history. Root inspects the full diff, reruns proof, handles records and final review/release. No overlapping writers.
* **Files:** `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs`, `src-tauri/src/game.rs`, `src-tauri/src/main.rs` only for shutdown integration coverage if needed, `src-tauri/src/chess.rs` only for existing command-boundary coverage if needed, and `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md` plus `CHESS_LOGIC_MAP.md` for material ownership-contract documentation. Use existing `src-tauri/src/error.rs` representation without a serialized type change.
* **Contract:** O1–O5. The executor chooses private helper names, state representation and fixture structure. Do not split production failure fixes into a later phase.
* **Exact phase proof:** `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked engine::process::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked game::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked shutdown` then `pnpm checks:pre-review`. All commands must pass. New assertions for each obligation must be shown red with only the corresponding production fix removed, then green after restoration. Keep fixtures and fault controls target-scoped, without sleep-based correctness assertions.
* **Cumulative review:** correctness and root-cause, plus engine protocol, platform semantics, error handling, tests, minimalism, and other description-triggered lenses over the final diff. No plan-only review is reused to approve code.
* **Final release:** project push skill, `pnpm gates:push -- --rust`, existing remote-CI refusal check, verified non-force master push, required `rust-windows-test`, `rust-macos-test` and `rust-platform` jobs, then the clean verified-SHA local install. Add `--frontend --bindings` only if the actual diff changes cross-layer contracts. No deploy.

# Plan: Retain failed engine cleanup ownership

## Goal

Resolve f-20261001-14. A failed engine teardown stays discoverable and retryable by its exact native generation. Game replacement and application shutdown cannot report successful cleanup after the production game loop lost a failed reap.

## MANDATE

### A failed engine termination drops the actor from the supervisor, so no later owner can retry or confirm cleanup

* **ID:** f-20261001-14 · **Status:** open · **Area:** engine-uci · **Root:** - · **Entry:** build · **Blocked:** none
* **Where:** `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:1448-1461` (`terminate_exact`: `let result = current.actor.terminate().await; self.actors.remove(key); result`), used by `retire_engine`, `retire_executables`, `kill_engine`, `kill_engines` and tab termination.
* **Defect:** when `actor.terminate()` returns an error, the actor is removed from the registry anyway. The child process may still be alive (kill-on-drop is best effort), and nothing — tab close, app shutdown via `RunEvent::ExitRequested`, a later retirement — can find it again.
* **Why it matters:** `.claude/rules/async-resource-invariants.md` requires cleanup on every exit path; an engine with a large hash (8 GB in Felix's configuration) can outlive its owner unseen.
* **Open question:** keep failed actors in a bounded "reaping" set that shutdown and later retirements retry, or make termination escalate (kill, wait with deadline) until it is confirmed before removal — and how the renderer learns that a process could not be confirmed dead.
* **Found by:** Codex `review-engine-protocol` lens, round 2 of the Stockfish 19 upgrade plan review (`tasks/plans/2026-10-01-stockfish-19-upgrade.md`, issue R2 retirement-failure), confirmed by the orchestrator from source, 2026-10-01.
* **Additional review evidence (2026-10-09):** f-20260926-07 cumulative review traced the normally joined production game loop. src-tauri/src/game.rs logs a terminate_game_engines failure at the loop tail and returns (). Shared pending teardown subsequently removes the failed generation, so finish_retired's unconditional exact termination finds no registered or pending owner and returns Ok. This can make replacement and shutdown_all report success after an unconfirmed reap. Existing clean-join failure tests use a loop that skips engine cleanup and do not exercise that production loss path. The cause is this finding's failed-actor/result retention contract, not an empty-join cancellation gap. Preserve the completed outcome or failed generation until the owning game/shutdown path has observed failure. Verification for this finding must include the production-loop cleanup path, not only direct fallback teardown. The separate design question remains open at build tier. review-error-handling confidence 95, adopting drain a51461c5-317b-40c0-8297-ab16a2fc6904.

## Threat model and non-goals

Ordinary user actions overlap teardown, replacement, tab close, engine removal and shutdown. An engine can be silent, exit unexpectedly, or fail an OS kill or wait. Callers can be cancelled while teardown proceeds. Support Linux, macOS and Windows. A permanently unkillable OS process cannot be guaranteed dead within a finite deadline, so retain its authority and report the unconfirmed outcome honestly.

Do not add an adversarial child-descendant threat model, PID-based signalling, new renderer controls, IPC events, persistent cleanup journals, configurable retry policy, or a background retry service. Warm-search ownership, closed-tab admission and post-shutdown late publication remain their separately recorded work. Planner co-located joiners are not planned here.

## Traced premises

Base: d445b1280ac4061aa1c54506a500bcfa6c827551. Entry revalidation: build remains required. The actor's task completion is distinct from confirming the child exited, and recovery ownership plus failure observation needs a shared contract.

* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:984-1026`: termination already bounds quit/write/wait and force-kill/reap. More escalation alone cannot guarantee success.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:762-798,1207-1213`: `ChildUciIo` owns the child control and consumes it for termination, including failure. Keeping only a finished actor cannot retry its OS wait.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2171-2222,2409-2457`: exact termination hands registered actors to pending ownership, with generation checks and preemption before lifecycle waiting.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2604-2659,2733-2814`: drains scan registered and pending actors, but the entry-owned termination task removes a pending entry for both success and failure. Leaving a failed entry there without changing the drain loop would spin forever.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:3634-3651,4034-4049,4288-4348`: the first actor teardown outcome and shared task join are already retained per actor. They are not a retryable child owner or a supervisor-discoverable failed-generation record.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2119,3220-3275`: the production interactive and shared registered launch paths spawn before installing pending ownership. Slot reservation and cleanup transfer must precede child creation rather than refuse ownership after it.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:3389-3444`: incomplete child IO construction has bounded cleanup too. A failed cleanup cannot be discarded merely because no complete actor exists yet.
* `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1147-1215,1428-1464,3111-3121`: the real game loop only logs engine cleanup failure, while retirement later retries exact registrations and may see no entry. Its joined task returns unit.
* `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1536-1563,2021-2055`: natural completion removes a session from `games`. Shutdown rescans failed sessions without retiring them, so a retained failure also needs a once-per-session drain rule.
* `src-tauri/src/main.rs:1720,1823-1844`: exit runs game and supervisor teardown concurrently inside a 15-second process budget. No sequential retry sweep may extend that budget.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:11120-11133`: an existing test locks in removal after a failed teardown and needs to assert failed-owner retention instead.
* `src-tauri/src/game.rs:1181`, `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:4338`, `src-tauri/src/error.rs:271,959-970`: actor and game task joins interpolate a raw `JoinError` into renderer-serialized Conflict text. Locked Tokio 1.50.0 `runtime/task/error.rs:134-149` includes a string panic payload in Display. Source confirms the diagnostic egress, runtime proof is pending.

## Approach

### O1 — Own the child until confirmed reaped

One shared cleanup owner carries the original child handle, executable/resource leases and exact generation across runtime teardown and actor-task exit. Route initial graceful teardown and later forced cleanup through the existing bounded child termination domain primitive. Successful OS wait is the condition that releases the child authority. Failed kill, failed wait, timeout, cancelled requester and incomplete IO construction preserve a supervisor-reachable cleanup owner. Keep protocol service permanently closed after termination starts. Actor completion must not hold a request open waiting for an unbounded reaper.

Verification level: deterministic child-control tests plus a real child fixture. After an injected failed wait, assert the same owned child can be reaped on a later bounded attempt and that successful reaping releases ownership. Keeping the old consume-and-drop implementation must fail this assertion. Test partial IO construction through its production cleanup boundary.

### O2 — Retain failed generations and share each attempt

Use the existing generation-keyed pending ownership, without a second cleanup registry. A failed attempt leaves its entry discoverable, cancelled and unusable for new UCI work. All exact and matching teardown operations, replacement cleanup, rejected publication and dropped-owner cleanup use this ownership. A later explicit teardown invocation may start one bounded retry of a completed failed attempt. Concurrent callers attached to one attempt receive that attempt's identical result, even if a later attempt recovers. Requester cancellation cannot cancel the owned attempt. Retain actor-join failure separately from child-reap confirmation so a successful child retry does not erase an actor task error.

Each drain processes a generation at most once per invocation, including generations handed off while scanning. A failed retained entry cannot cause an immediate repeat or infinite loop. Preserve the first generation's identity if the same key acquires a newer actor. Exact cleanup never touches another generation. Matching tab/id/executable drains include older failed entries even after renderer removal. Remove the pending entry only after confirmed child cleanup and safe terminal-result publication to attached owners.

Verification level: supervisor integration tests. A failed exact teardown followed by a tab/global/identity drain must return the retained failure or the result of a real bounded retry, and the owner remains until confirmation. Controlled concurrent terminators and cancelled waiters must observe one attempt, with original error category, display, diagnostic and root failure intact. A repeated-failure drain must return within its deadline and leave ownership. Same-name/path engines and a newer generation must remain independent.

### O3 — Bound ownership without evicting unresolved children

Reserve capacity before every production supervisor-owned child creation. The native limit is 4096 concurrently owned child generations, including active, spawning and unresolved cleanup. Capacity follows the child rather than a mutable key or replaceable admission. Full capacity refuses a new spawn with the existing renderer-safe `ResourceLimit`, without creating a child or deleting a failed owner. An admission abandoned before spawn releases its reservation. After spawning, only confirmed cleanup releases the reservation. No eviction or expiration of a live or unconfirmed child. This bound prevents an endless sequence of failed retirements accumulating handles and processes.

Verification level: capacity tests with a smaller test-only limit. Assert refusal happens before the production spawn boundary, unresolved teardown keeps its slot, pre-spawn cancellation returns its slot, and confirmed reaping makes capacity available. Exercise concurrent admission and key replacement so map removal cannot prematurely return capacity.

### O4 — Preserve the production game cleanup outcome

The live game session retains the production loop's engine cleanup outcome, including on natural completion. Retirement, replacement and shutdown that own the session observe that outcome even if a later supervisor retry succeeds or the loop has already joined. Combine it with fallback cleanup and join failures using the existing shared error representation. Both game sides are attempted. Concurrent game and supervisor shutdown must share generation teardown without consuming each other's failure. Natural completion keeps an unobserved failed session reachable in existing game ownership, while completed-game snapshots and events keep their current meaning. The first completed retirement observation may release this failed-session ownership, even when returning the failure, because unresolved children remain supervisor-owned under O2. Callers already holding the session still receive its retained result. Bound unobserved failed-session retention at 4096, reserving its place before game launch and refusing overflow before resources are created, without silently pruning failures. Game shutdown processes a captured session once per invocation and cannot rescan a failed session indefinitely.

Verification level: actual game-loop integration tests. Drive the production loop through its cleanup tail with injected reap failure, then retire/replace/shut down. Assert the owning caller receives that failure, including after a successful later retry. Keep normal natural completion, join timeout/abort and stale-session replacement green. A stand-in loop that skips production cleanup cannot satisfy this obligation.

### O5 — Keep existing error and exit contracts

Direct renderer commands return stable existing error categories for unconfirmed cleanup. Native logs carry generation and diagnostic context, while OS paths and raw diagnostic details stay native. Actor and game task-join failures use one shared renderer-safe conversion, retaining native diagnostic detail in the owning log. Never interpolate raw JoinError text into a public Conflict. Keep the existing serialized ErrorPayload shape and its consumers. No new binding, event, UI control or capability scope is required. Verify the existing consumer paths for Kill, tab close, removal and game replacement rather than creating another notification route. Global shutdown reports failure through its existing aggregation and remains within `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET`, even with retained failed entries and concurrent game cleanup.
Committed game abort retains its existing public `Ok` contract after the abort has committed. Its cleanup failure remains logged and supervisor-owned, without making the already committed action appear uncommitted.

Verification level: command/error serialization tests and native shutdown integration tests. For actor and game task panics carrying a unique private-path marker, assert serialized results and composite cleanup errors omit that marker while native logs retain diagnostic context. Assert unconfirmed cleanup cannot produce `Ok` or a successful native shutdown result, and that repeated failures do not extend the bounded process exit path. Use cross-platform deterministic child controls for the ownership protocol, real process proof on Unix, Windows GNU compile locally and the required Windows/macOS CI runtime jobs before release. After cumulative code review and repairs, build the release app through `pnpm gate:ensure tauri-build`, then run `pnpm verify:app`. This proves the actual product's normal IPC and close-control exit, without claiming to exercise a live engine registered through the native picker. Failed-reap retry and engine process facts remain covered by the native real-child and actual game-loop tests. No custom verifier or picker bypass is required.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Optimal: retain native cleanup authority with bounded explicit retries → I recommend this approach.

Decided autonomously for adopter recording:

* Keep the existing pending registry as the reaping owner, extending its failed-state contract. Reject a second registry, unlimited automatic retries and mere failed-actor retention after its child handle was dropped. Reverse by substituting a proven shared cleanup owner with the same O1–O5 semantics.
* Retain the child handle after unconfirmed cleanup. This changes d-20260901-18's drop-after-timeout clause and its keep-actor rejection, with new evidence: the actor must still exit, but cleanup authority can live independently, and this finding demonstrates lost ownership after that drop. Keep its bounded waits and rejection of detached unbounded waits. The adopter records the partial correction explicitly before implementation.
* Preserve d-20261006-16/19's pending owner, cancellation independence and lossless result sharing. Extend them to multiple explicit attempts after failure, justified by this finding's unconfirmed cleanup. Preserve d-20261007-17/19's exact-generation and Kill handoff rules. Preserve d-20261008-10/11 preemption and stable requester error semantics. Refine d-20261008-12 by separating the original actor ending result from later child cleanup attempts, never overwrite what an attached original caller observed.
* Reserve 4096 owned-generation slots before spawning to bound non-evictable cleanup owners. Reject failure-only insertion with eviction or refusal after a child exists. This is a native safety bound, with no setting or extra tool. Reverse by replacing reservation with an equally proven finite ownership bound.
* Retain the real game-loop outcome on its live session. Reject log-only failure and relying on a later empty registry scan. Reverse by making the actual loop's joined return value equivalently carry cleanup failure to every session owner.
* Replace raw actor/game JoinError interpolation with a shared stable public conversion and native-only detailed logs. This corrects an evidenced existing leak at both integrations rather than preserving it as part of error compatibility. The serialized shape and existing category remain stable. Reject a new public error variant or duplicate join mappers. Reversal would require an equally proven public/native diagnostic separation, never reintroducing panic text into serialized messages.

No Felix product decision is pending. These choices repair the existing teardown contract, without adding a user workflow.

## Risks / open questions

* Some OS waits never confirm exit. A bounded retry returns failure and retains ownership until another explicit cleanup or process exit. Do not claim success from `start_kill` or kill-on-drop.
* Actor join errors and original cleanup errors must remain observable after a recovery. Avoid reusing a first-outcome `OnceLock` as a mutable retry result.
* Child handles and executable/resource leases must move together. Reaping by a saved PID is forbidden because identity can be reused.
* Planner proof is source tracing and adversarial review only. Runtime tests, fault-revert evidence, gates and platform CI are obligations for implementation, not results claimed here.

## Not part of this task

Co-located findings, f-20261006-04 late publication, unrelated protocol timing, renderer storage, engine-cache behaviour, legal texts, deployment and installer changes. The adopter handles eligible joiners after this plan under next-finding, with their own proofs.

## Phases

One cohesive engine-cleanup ownership phase. Child recovery, pending attempts, capacity and game-result observation must land together because their ownership/result contracts are coupled. There are no independent dependency waves to split across overlapping source files.

* **Ownership:** one selected-executor workspace-write leaf, role `sensitive`, with a self-contained reviewed brief and no root history. Root inspects the full diff, reruns proof, handles records and final review/release. No overlapping writers.
* **Files:** `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs`, `src-tauri/src/game.rs`, `src-tauri/src/error.rs` for the shared safe task-join conversion and its coverage, `src-tauri/src/main.rs` only for shutdown integration coverage if needed, `src-tauri/src/chess.rs` only for existing command-boundary coverage if needed, and `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md` plus `CHESS_LOGIC_MAP.md` for material ownership-contract documentation. Preserve the existing serialized error shape.
* **Contract:** O1–O5. The executor chooses private helper names, state representation and fixture structure. Do not split production failure fixes into a later phase.
* **Exact phase proof:** `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked engine::process::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked game::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked error::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked chess::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked shutdown` then `pnpm checks:pre-review`. All commands must pass. New assertions for each obligation must be shown red with only the corresponding production fix removed, then green after restoration. Keep fixtures and fault controls target-scoped, without sleep-based correctness assertions.
* **Cumulative review:** correctness and root-cause, plus engine protocol, platform semantics, error handling, tests, minimalism, and other description-triggered lenses over the final diff. No plan-only review is reused to approve code.
* **Final release:** project push skill, `pnpm gates:push -- --rust` when only backend implementation files changed. If `src-tauri/src/main.rs` changes, including tests, or another cross-layer contract changes, the project mapping requires `pnpm gates:push -- --rust --frontend --bindings`. Preserve the real-app verification above, existing remote-CI refusal check, verified non-force master push, required `rust-windows-test`, `rust-macos-test` and `rust-platform` jobs, then the clean verified-SHA local install. No deploy.

* **I1 — Raw task panic diagnostics can reach the renderer.** Witness: review-tauri-security, round 1, raw finding 1, blocker, confidence 98. After spawning, only confirmed cleanup releases the reservation. No eviction or expiration of a live or unconfirmed child. This bound prevents an endless sequence of failed retirements accumulating handles and processes.

Verification level: capacity tests with a smaller test-only limit. Assert refusal happens before the production spawn boundary, unresolved teardown keeps its slot, pre-spawn cancellation returns its slot, and confirmed reaping makes capacity available. Exercise concurrent admission and key replacement so map removal cannot prematurely return capacity.

### O4 — Preserve the production game cleanup outcome

The live game session retains the production loop's engine cleanup outcome, including on natural completion. Retirement, replacement and shutdown that own the session observe that outcome even if a later supervisor retry succeeds or the loop has already joined. Combine it with fallback cleanup and join failures using the existing shared error representation. Both game sides are attempted. Concurrent game and supervisor shutdown must share generation teardown without consuming each other's failure. Natural completion keeps an unobserved failed session reachable in existing game ownership, while completed-game snapshots and events keep their current meaning. The first completed retirement observation may release this failed-session ownership, even when returning the failure, because unresolved children remain supervisor-owned under O2. Callers already holding the session still receive its retained result. Bound unobserved failed-session retention at 4096, reserving its place before game launch and refusing overflow before resources are created, without silently pruning failures. Game shutdown processes a captured session once per invocation and cannot rescan a failed session indefinitely.

Verification level: actual game-loop integration tests. Drive the production loop through its cleanup tail with injected reap failure, then retire/replace/shut down. Assert the owning caller receives that failure, including after a successful later retry. Keep normal natural completion, join timeout/abort and stale-session replacement green. A stand-in loop that skips production cleanup cannot satisfy this obligation.

### O5 — Keep existing error and exit contracts

Direct renderer commands return stable existing error categories for unconfirmed cleanup. Native logs carry generation and diagnostic context, while OS paths and raw diagnostic details stay native. Actor and game task-join failures use one shared renderer-safe conversion, retaining native diagnostic detail in the owning log. Never interpolate raw JoinError text into a public Conflict. Keep the existing serialized ErrorPayload shape and its consumers. No new binding, event, UI control or capability scope is required. Verify the existing consumer paths for Kill, tab close, removal and game replacement rather than creating another notification route. Global shutdown reports failure through its existing aggregation and remains within `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET`, even with retained failed entries and concurrent game cleanup.
Committed game abort retains its existing public `Ok` contract after the abort has committed. Its cleanup failure remains logged and supervisor-owned, without making the already committed action appear uncommitted.

Verification level: command/error serialization tests and native shutdown integration tests. For actor and game task panics carrying a unique private-path marker, assert serialized results and composite cleanup errors omit that marker while native logs retain diagnostic context. Assert unconfirmed cleanup cannot produce `Ok` or a successful native shutdown result, and that repeated failures do not extend the bounded process exit path. Use cross-platform deterministic child controls for the ownership protocol, real process proof on Unix, Windows GNU compile locally and the required Windows/macOS CI runtime jobs before release. After cumulative code review and repairs, build the release app through `pnpm gate:ensure tauri-build`, then run `pnpm verify:app`. This proves the actual product's normal IPC and close-control exit, without claiming to exercise a live engine registered through the native picker. Failed-reap retry and engine process facts remain covered by the native real-child and actual game-loop tests. No custom verifier or picker bypass is required.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Optimal: retain native cleanup authority with bounded explicit retries → I recommend this approach.

Decided autonomously for adopter recording:

* Keep the existing pending registry as the reaping owner, extending its failed-state contract. Reject a second registry, unlimited automatic retries and mere failed-actor retention after its child handle was dropped. Reverse by substituting a proven shared cleanup owner with the same O1–O5 semantics.
* Retain the child handle after unconfirmed cleanup. This changes d-20260901-18's drop-after-timeout clause and its keep-actor rejection, with new evidence: the actor must still exit, but cleanup authority can live independently, and this finding demonstrates lost ownership after that drop. Keep its bounded waits and rejection of detached unbounded waits. The adopter records the partial correction explicitly before implementation.
* Preserve d-20261006-16/19's pending owner, cancellation independence and lossless result sharing. Extend them to multiple explicit attempts after failure, justified by this finding's unconfirmed cleanup. Preserve d-20261007-17/19's exact-generation and Kill handoff rules. Preserve d-20261008-10/11 preemption and stable requester error semantics. Refine d-20261008-12 by separating the original actor ending result from later child cleanup attempts, never overwrite what an attached original caller observed.
* Reserve 4096 owned-generation slots before spawning to bound non-evictable cleanup owners. Reject failure-only insertion with eviction or refusal after a child exists. This is a native safety bound, with no setting or extra tool. Reverse by replacing reservation with an equally proven finite ownership bound.
* Retain the real game-loop outcome on its live session. Reject log-only failure and relying on a later empty registry scan. Reverse by making the actual loop's joined return value equivalently carry cleanup failure to every session owner.
* Replace raw actor/game JoinError interpolation with a shared stable public conversion and native-only detailed logs. This corrects an evidenced existing leak at both integrations rather than preserving it as part of error compatibility. The serialized shape and existing category remain stable. Reject a new public error variant or duplicate join mappers. Reversal would require an equally proven public/native diagnostic separation, never reintroducing panic text into serialized messages.

No Felix product decision is pending. These choices repair the existing teardown contract, without adding a user workflow.

## Risks / open questions

* Some OS waits never confirm exit. A bounded retry returns failure and retains ownership until another explicit cleanup or process exit. Do not claim success from `start_kill` or kill-on-drop.
* Actor join errors and original cleanup errors must remain observable after a recovery. Avoid reusing a first-outcome `OnceLock` as a mutable retry result.
* Child handles and executable/resource leases must move together. Reaping by a saved PID is forbidden because identity can be reused.
* Planner proof is source tracing and adversarial review only. Runtime tests, fault-revert evidence, gates and platform CI are obligations for implementation, not results claimed here.

## Not part of this task

Co-located findings, f-20261006-04 late publication, unrelated protocol timing, renderer storage, engine-cache behaviour, legal texts, deployment and installer changes. The adopter handles eligible joiners after this plan under next-finding, with their own proofs.

One cohesive engine-cleanup ownership phase. Child recovery, pending attempts, capacity and game-result observation must land together because their ownership/result contracts are coupled. There are no independent dependency waves to split across overlapping source files.

* **Ownership:** one selected-executor workspace-write leaf, role `sensitive`, with a self-contained reviewed brief and no root history. Root inspects the full diff, reruns proof, handles records and final review/release. No overlapping writers.
* **Files:** `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs`, `src-tauri/src/game.rs`, `src-tauri/src/error.rs` for the shared safe task-join conversion and its coverage, `src-tauri/src/main.rs` only for shutdown integration coverage if needed, `src-tauri/src/chess.rs` only for existing command-boundary coverage if needed, and `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md` plus `CHESS_LOGIC_MAP.md` for material ownership-contract documentation. Preserve the existing serialized error shape.
* **Contract:** O1–O5. The executor chooses private helper names, state representation and fixture structure. Do not split production failure fixes into a later phase.
* **Exact phase proof:** `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked engine::process::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked game::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked error::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked chess::tests` then `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked shutdown` then `pnpm checks:pre-review`. All commands must pass. New assertions for each obligation must be shown red with only the corresponding production fix removed, then green after restoration. Keep fixtures and fault controls target-scoped, without sleep-based correctness assertions.
* **Cumulative review:** correctness and root-cause, plus engine protocol, platform semantics, error handling, tests, minimalism, and other description-triggered lenses over the final diff. No plan-only review is reused to approve code.
* **Final release:** project push skill, `pnpm gates:push -- --rust` when only backend implementation files changed. If `src-tauri/src/main.rs` changes, including tests, or another cross-layer contract changes, the project mapping requires `pnpm gates:push -- --rust --frontend --bindings`. Preserve the real-app verification above, existing remote-CI refusal check, verified non-force master push, required `rust-windows-test`, `rust-macos-test` and `rust-platform` jobs, then the clean verified-SHA local install. No deploy.

#### lens-correctness-r1 — complete terminal report

[blocker] src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2283 — Concurrent exact terminators can receive different attempt results. A holds the lifecycle lock during teardown. B arrives during that attempt and waits here. If A fails, B subsequently starts a retry through `reap_published`. When that retry succeeds, B returns `Ok` because `tracked` suppresses the original actor outcome. O2 requires concurrent terminators to share the original attempt and its failure. (confidence: 96)

[blocker] src-tauri/src/game.rs:1256 — Concurrent retirement can report success before the game task finishes. A takes the running join handle. B, already holding the same session, sees an empty join slot and no recorded join failure. With no registered engines, B reaches `outcome()`, whose unset value becomes `Ok`, and releases the observation slot. If the task then panics, only A receives the failure. O4 requires every attached session owner to observe the retained outcome. (confidence: 94)

[blocker] src/components/puzzles/Puzzles.tsx:258 — Rejected puzzle setup still publishes an invalid puzzle to the board. #### lens-engine-protocol-r1 — complete terminal report

[blocker] src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:2282 — Concurrent exact terminators attach only after acquiring the lifecycle lock, so they can receive different cleanup outcomes. Concrete sequence: an engine-retirement drain starts teardown while the game ends. The production loop calls `terminate_exact` for that same generation and waits behind the drain. The first reap fails and leaves the pending owner retained. When the loop acquires the lock, `reap_published` calls `start_termination`, which starts a new attempt because the first attempt has completed. If that retry succeeds, the loop receives `Ok`, records successful cleanup and removes the completed session. Its original concurrent cleanup failure is lost to later replacement or shutdown. The `!tracked` fallback does not help because the failed pending generation remains tracked. This violates O2’s shared concurrent-attempt outcome and O4’s production-game failure retention. ### Complete t1 mechanism note

```text
# Live puzzle timing writers and move equivalence

Candidate t1, a new focused correction judgment after D4, not a revision of q1 text.

## Mandate and surviving contract

D4 correctness reports the timer-effect cleanup can overwrite the first completion time after changeCompletion claims it. D4 chess-semantics reports equivalent legal castling gestures are rejected by literal UCI comparison, also used in progress. Both are authorized Fix in the live puzzle consumer scope. Preserve d-20261014-19/20/21, all D1-D3 closures and current saved-session deferral. This note repairs those implementation gaps without changing persisted formats or product policy.

## Timing ownership

Completion and elapsed time are one authoritative first transition. Every elapsed-time writer, including effect cleanup, must honor the live puzzle record identity and completion. A cleanup may preserve elapsed time only on the exact still-incomplete record it owns. It cannot write over a completed record, a removed record or a different record reusing the index. Preserve existing incomplete timing behavior on selection, tracking changes and unmount, alongside first-completion stability. Trace the real storage setter and all timer writers, rather than relying on a captured isPuzzleIncomplete boolean or assuming deferred updater execution. No second ended flag or timer registry.

## Move equivalence

Move comparison uses chessops normalization in the parent position. Board acceptance and shared ordered progress use one domain comparison, retaining legal-move admission and ordered cursor occurrence/frontier semantics. Both normal king-destination and king-to-rook representations of legal castling are equivalent. Ordinary moves and promotions remain distinct as appropriate. Preserve actual accepted move recording, alternate-mate behavior, atomic pair rejection, root/no-last-move hint behavior and frontier-only play. Reuse the installed library normalization, no custom castling parser or new tree-store implementation.

## Boundary and failure semantics

Writable scope remains the four puzzle component/test files and existing src/utils/puzzles.ts, optional utility test only if useful. No shared tree/atom/storage implementation, key/version/schema, native IPC, localization/copy or new completion status. Ownership-loss and old metadata behavior remain quiet. Saved-session reconciliation remains separate. Incorrect moves remain recorded as variations under the existing contract.

## Verification level

Actual Puzzles, Board, store/parser and MoveControls consumer tests must expose post-completion cleanup timing after the clock advances, old record/index replacement, incomplete cleanup preservation and normalized castling acceptance/progress under both legal encodings and nonstandard starts. Preserve q1 occurrence/frontier, mate, playback, metadata and admission proof. Independently restore the unguarded cleanup and literal Board/progress comparison in production with unchanged test bytes, require distinguishable runtime assertion exit1, exact restoration and green0. Run focused and importer/utility consumers, types and warning-denying file lint. Root combined pre-review precedes committed closure review, browser and final gates.
```

### Complete t2 mechanism note

```text
# Puzzle progress after branch reordering

Candidate t2, a new focused judgment after D5. The approved q1/t1 contracts and complete prior history remain governing except q1's implementation premise that the matching frontier must be found only from root child0.

## Mandate

D5 chess-semantics proves notation promotion reorders an already-recorded correct solution branch. Replaying the correct pair reuses that branch, leaving cursor[0,1,0] which child0-only progress rejects. D5 minimalism also identifies canonical uciNormalize and a second BoardGame normalization expression. Both are authorized Fix. Existing timing, mate, ownership, atomic-admission and saved-session boundaries survive.

## Actual ordered cursor and frontier

Recognize the current cursor's exact ordered path from the declared puzzle root, comparing each actual branch move with its corresponding solution occurrence. Child indices are location, not correctness. Root identity, legal parent-position equivalence and a fully resolved path remain required. Repeated tokens and nonstandard starts retain their relative occurrence semantics.

Frontier-only play remains: do not enable replay at an earlier cursor with an already-recorded matching mainline continuation. Derive that matching continuation relative to the actual matched cursor/branch, so a correct solution node remains playable after another branch was promoted. A mismatched promoted child cannot make a correctly matched alternative path invalid. Hints at matched earlier cursors preserve existing behavior, root and terminal positions retain no hint. Unmatched move paths remain unplayable and offer no hint. Preserve user notation promotion and valid trees/variations, no reset or forced global reordering to hide the mismatch.

## Canonical conversion and boundary

Keep one puzzle-specific comparison for expected-token parsing, legality and equivalence. Its normalized-UCI conversion and BoardGame's matching-child conversion use existing uciNormalize. Preserve BoardGame's native castling synchronization. Choose compatible existing types without casts or unnecessary broad API changes. No new abstraction or registry.

Writable scope: existing puzzle component/test files and src/utils/puzzles.ts, plus BoardGame.tsx and its existing test if needed. Existing src/utils/chess.ts may change only if a type adaptation is genuinely required for canonical conversion. No shared TreeStore implementation, storage key/version/schema, native IPC, completion status or user-facing copy change. This is a live path-recognition repair, not saved-session reconciliation or a global mainline-option redesign.

## Verification level

Actual Puzzles/Board/notation actions and store/parser consumers must exercise wrong c5, correct e5/Nf3, real promotion of c5, navigation and replay, then usable hint/Nc6 at the reused solution endpoint. Assert preserved wrong branch, correct cursor/solution outcome, occurrence and frontier semantics. Include reorder while already on the correct branch, invalid/unmatched branches, nonstandard/castling and earlier-cursor blocking. Existing BoardGame native castling promotion regression proves unchanged synchronization after canonical-helper reuse.

Reverse only actual production path recognition to its old child0-only premise with unchanged tests, require distinguishable runtime exit1, exact restoration and green0. Reusing canonical conversion is behavior-preserving, do not manufacture a failing behavioral reversal for the equivalent duplicate. Run affected consumer/importer tests, types and warning-denying file lint. Root combined pre-review precedes committed closure review, browser and final release gates. No new persistence claim.
```
